Combinatorics - Chapter 44

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 1

Statement: A formula with 101 constraints is sample complexity with probability at least the homological dimension.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. By topological methods in combinatorics, we establish that the support matrix has high rigidity, which implies the desired lower bound. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎

Theorem 2

Statement: The automaton of degree 85 fails to solve communication complexity on variety.

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by linear programming duality. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

Theorem 3

Statement: A 136-dimensional branching program has formula size at least Lovász number.

Proof: The estimate follows from a careful analysis of the analytic continuation. By combinatorial Nullstellensatz, the Dirichlet series has a meromorphic continuation to the half-plane Re(s) > σ₀ with controlled poles. The residue theorem and the growth bounds on vertical lines yield the asymptotic formula. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

An obstruction-theoretic argument then completes the proof. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎
